      162  ---
      163  
      164  ## 2. Why this topology
      165  
      137 -**One rule shaped all of it:** durable facts, short-lived inte
          -ntions, and Postgres decides.                                 
      166 +### 2.1 I measured first                                      
      167  
      139 -### D1. Commands by request/reply, facts by JetStream, claim b
          -efore run                                                     
      168 +Before writing any code, I cut a bare NATS client off the netw
          +ork, with a peer that stayed                                  
      169 +connected. Every second it sent a request (2s timeout) and a J
          +etStream publish (5s timeout).                                
      170 +Runs: 6s and 20s cuts, with default pings and with 2s pings. *
          +*My prediction was wrong                                      
      171 +twice** (measured, C1):                                       
      172  
      141 -**Chosen:**                                                   
      142 -- **Commands** are core request/reply.                        
      143 -- **Facts** go through JetStream.                             
      144 -- **The driver claims** a job, under the same row lock as a ca
          -ncel, before running it.                                      
      173 +- **Requests that timed out were still delivered,** up to 20s 
          +late. With default pings the                                  
      174 +  TCP connection survives the cut, so nothing was lost, but ev
          +erything was late, including                                  
      175 +  requests the sender had already given up on.                
      176 +- **7 of 8 JetStream publishes that reported a timeout had in 
          +fact been stored.**                                           
      177  
      146 -**Instead of:**                                               
      147 -- *A, request/reply plus executor polling:* simplest. But whil
          -e the executor is down, facts                                 
      148 -  live only in driver memory and the ledger. It also assumes t
          -he instrument can always be                                   
      149 -  asked, which is true of this simulator and false of most equ
          -ipment.                                                       
      150 -- *B, JetStream both ways:* a durable command queue makes stal
          -e physical commands possible,                                 
      151 -  such as a dispense delivered after a cancel. It also forces 
          -AckWait tuning across jobs                                    
      152 -  that last from 2s to hours, with 30s hangs.                 
      153 -- *D, shared state in NATS KV:* a second source of truth besid
          -e Postgres, without                                           
      154 -  transactions.                                               
      155 -- *Command expiry or a cancel broadcast, instead of a claim:* 
          -both narrow the window for a                                  
      156 -  late command, but they race with the cancel, and expiry depe
          -nds on clocks.                                                
      178 +So **a timeout means "unknown", never "it didn't happen".** An
          +ything that treats a timeout as                               
      179 +"not delivered", such as "no reply, so start a new attempt", w
          +ould sooner or later run a job                                
      180 +twice or record a false outcome. The decisions below are what 
          +follows from that.                                            
      181  
      158 -**Why, measured first (C1).** I partitioned a bare NATS client
          - before writing any code, and                                 
      159 -my prediction was wrong twice:                                
      160 -- With default pings, nothing was lost but everything was late
          -. That included requests the                                  
      161 -  sender had already given up on, which arrived about 20s late
          -.                                                             
      162 -- 7 of 8 JetStream publishes that reported a timeout had in fa
          -ct been stored.                                               
      182 +### 2.2 Commands as requests, facts as a stream               
      183  
      164 -So a timeout means *unknown*, never "it didn't happen". That r
          -ules out "no reply, so send a                                 
      165 -new attempt" and anything that treats a timeout as "not delive
          -red". It's why the design                                     
      166 -has:                                                          
      167 -- the claim;                                                  
      168 -- resending the same `job_id`;                                
      169 -- Msg-Id plus idempotent apply;                               
      170 -- an explicit `PingInterval` (5s, 2 outstanding).             
      184 +**Problem.** How should commands reach the drivers, and outcom
          +es come back, so that the                                     
      185 +record stays true through lost, repeated and late messages?   
      186  
      172 -### Reconcile by resending, not by a query (S7)               
      187 +**Choice.** Commands are core NATS requests. Facts go through 
          +JetStream. Postgres decides.                                  
      188  
      174 -- **The case:** a step that is claimed with no evidence. The d
          -river died after its claim, or                                
      175 -  the instrument refused the job.                             
      176 -- **Chosen:** resend the same command.                        
      177 -- **Instead of:** a separate `job` query subject.             
      178 -- **Why:** the command already asks the driver what it knows o
          -f the job. Its idempotence                                    
      179 -  (`known` if the job is current, on the instrument, or in the
          - ledger) is what makes the                                    
      180 -  resend safe. A query would add a subject and driver code to 
          -learn nothing more.                                           
      181 -- **Measured live:** a driver killed after its claim was grant
          -ed but before it started. The                                 
      182 -  job ran exactly once after the restart.                     
      189 +| Option | How it works | Why not |                           
      190 +| --- | --- | --- |                                           
      191 +| **Chosen: requests for commands, a stream for facts** | as a
          +bove | |                                                      
      192 +| Requests only; the executor polls the drivers for outcomes |
          + simplest | While the executor is down, facts live only in dri
          +ver memory and the ledger. It also assumes the instrument can 
          +always be asked, which is true of this simulator but not of mo
          +st equipment |                                                
      193 +| JetStream both ways | commands in a durable queue | A stored
          + command can run late: a dispense after a cancel, or after the
          + plate was moved by hand. One ack timeout would have to fit jo
          +bs of 2 seconds and of hours |                                
      194 +| Shared state in NATS KV | drivers and executor read and writ
          +e it | A second source of truth beside Postgres, without trans
          +actions |                                                     
      195  
      184 -### D2. A verified completion is never undone                 
      196 +**Trade-offs.**                                               
      197  
      186 -- **Chosen:** if a later read-back no longer matches the ledge
          -r, the step stays `completed`                                 
      187 -  and the mismatch is reported.                               
      188 -- **Why:** "completed, data verified at T" stays true for ever
          -. Moving the status back would                                
      189 -  regress it after dependent steps had already run.           
      198 +- An extra round trip per job, for the claim (§2.3).          
      199 +- JetStream delivers **at least once, not exactly once.** A re
          +peat within 2 minutes is                                      
      200 +  dropped by its message id. After that, applying the same fac
          +t twice changes nothing,                                      
      201 +  because each fact is applied idempotently.                  
      202 +- When the executor is cut off, events in flight are redeliver
          +ed only after the 30s ack                                     
      203 +  timeout. An 8s cut delayed a run by 30s (measured, C4f). The
          + record was behind, never                                     
      204 +  false.                                                      
      205  
      191 -### D3. A read reported lost stays open                       
      206 +### 2.3 Claim before run                                      
      207  
      193 -- **Chosen:** `data_lost` makes the read *unresolved* (API `ru
          -nning`), not failed. The                                      
      194 -  executor keeps looking in storage, and a matching object com
          -pletes the read.                                              
      195 -- **Why:** a dead driver's upload can still land later, and no
          -thing bounds when.                                            
      196 -- **Found in C2:** the first version failed the read after a g
          -race period, and a late upload                                
      197 -  then contradicted the record.                               
      198 -- **Rejected alternative, making loss final with a create-only
          - write (`If-None-Match: *`):**                                
      199 -  SeaweedFS 3.62 ignores the header. A second conditional PUT 
          -returned 200 and overwrote                                    
      200 -  the object (measured).                                      
      208 +**Problem.** A command can arrive late, and a cancel can arriv
          +e at any moment. How do we                                    
      209 +make sure a job never starts after it was cancelled?          
      210  
      202 -### D4. Retry per cause and per device, bounded               
      211 +**Choice.** Before calling the instrument, the driver asks the
          + executor for permission (a                                   
      212 +*claim*). The executor records it under the step's row lock, t
          +he same lock a cancel takes.                                  
      213 +Exactly one of them gets the lock first:                      
      214  
      204 -**Chosen:** a new attempt needs a positive claim that repeatin
          -g is safe, from the source                                    
      205 -for its cause:                                                
      215 +- **The cancel first.** The claim is answered `abort`, and the
          + job never runs.                                              
      216 +- **The claim first.** The job may run. The cancel no longer s
          +tops it, because the driver                                   
      217 +  may already be starting it. Its outcome is recorded when the
          + events arrive.                                               
      218  
      207 -| Cause | Who says it is safe | Default |                     
      219 +If the executor doesn't answer (it is down, or the database fa
          +iled), the driver doesn't                                     
      220 +start. It asks again.                                         
      221 +                                                              
      222 +*Example.* A run is cancelled while `fill_buffer_plate` waits 
          +for the busy liquid handler.                                  
      223 +The liquid handler frees up a moment before the cancel, and th
          +e driver accepts the command.                                 
      224 +Its claim arrives just after the cancel commits, so it is answ
          +ered `abort`. The ledger never                                
      225 +shows the job.                                                
      226 +                                                              
      227 +**Why not the alternatives.** Commands that expire, or a broad
          +cast "cancel" message, only                                   
      228 +make the window for a late command smaller. Both still race wi
          +th the cancel, and expiry also                                
      229 +depends on synchronised clocks. The lock closes the window.   
      230 +                                                              
      231 +**Trade-off.** It rests on one executor answering claims (assu
          +med; see §5).                                                 
      232 +                                                              
      233 +### 2.4 A repeated message never becomes a new attempt        
      234 +                                                              
      235 +**Problem.** A step is claimed, but no `started` arrives. Did 
          +the driver die before starting,                               
      236 +did the instrument refuse the job, or is the event just late? 
          +Guessing wrong either loses a                                 
      237 +job or runs it twice.                                         
      238 +                                                              
      239 +**Choice.** Keep sending the **same command, with the same job
          + id**, every 3s. The driver's                                 
      240 +answer is the evidence:                                       
      241 +                                                              
      242 +- **`known`:** the driver has the job, in flight or in its led
          +ger. It ran, or is running.                                   
      243 +  Wait for its events.                                        
      244 +- **`accepted`:** the driver has no record of it. The instrume
          +nt writes its ledger before                                   
      245 +  doing anything physical (inferred, `instrument.go`), so the 
          +job never started. The driver                                 
      246 +  claims it again, the executor answers `go`, and it runs once
          +.                                                             
      247 +- **No answer:** still unknown. The step stays `running` and t
          +he command is sent again.                                     
      248 +                                                              
      249 +*Example* (measured, PR #18): the plate-reader driver was kill
          +ed after its claim was                                        
      250 +granted, before it started the job. The step said `running` wi
          +th no start time. After the                                   
      251 +restart the resend was accepted, and the ledger shows the job 
          +ran exactly once.                                             
      252 +                                                              
      253 +A new attempt, with a new job id, is only ever started by the 
          +retry policy (§2.6), never by                                 
      254 +a timeout. I first designed a separate "what do you know about
          + this job?" query. The command                                
      255 +already asks exactly that, so the query would have added a sub
          +ject and driver code to learn                                 
      256 +nothing more.                                                 
      257 +                                                              
      258 +**Trade-off.** If a driver never comes back, its step stays `r
          +unning` for ever: we can't                                    
      259 +know whether it ran. An operator action to declare it lost was
          + not built (§5).                                              
      260 +                                                              
      261 +### 2.5 A read is complete only when its data is verified     
      262 +                                                              
      263 +**Problem.** The instrument hands a read's bytes to the driver
          + only in memory. The ledger                                   
      264 +saying `completed` doesn't mean the data is safe.             
      265 +                                                              
      266 +**Choice.** A read is `completed` only when the executor has r
          +ead the object back from the                                  
      267 +key it computes itself, and its sha256 equals the ledger's. Un
          +til then the step is `running`.                               
      268 +The driver keeps the plate reader busy until the upload succee
          +ds, so its bytes aren't                                       
      269 +dropped.                                                      
      270 +                                                              
      271 +**Once verified, it stays verified.** If a later read-back doe
          +sn't match (say the object was                                
      272 +corrupted), the step stays `completed` and the mismatch is rep
          +orted in `error`. Moving the                                  
      273 +status backwards would be true, but steps after it may already
          + have used that data.                                         
      274 +                                                              
      275 +### 2.6 Retry only when repeating is known to be safe         
      276 +                                                              
      277 +**Problem.** Some steps can't succeed as they stand: the instr
          +ument failed, hung, or a read's                               
      278 +data was lost. Without retries, every fault fails the run. A c
          +areless retry, though, could                                  
      279 +dispense liquid twice into the same plate.                    
      280 +                                                              
      281 +**Choice.** A new attempt needs a positive reason to think rep
          +eating is safe, from the right                                
      282 +source for its cause:                                         
      283 +                                                              
      284 +| Cause | Who says repeating is safe | Default |              
      285  | --- | --- | --- |
      209 -| Failure | The instrument's `retryable` flag | Liquid handler
          -: no. Incubator and plate reader: yes |                       
      210 -| Hang | Per-device setting `REPEAT_SAFE` | Plate reader only 
          -|                                                             
      211 -| Data lost | Per-device setting `REREAD_ON_DATA_LOSS` | Plate
          - reader only |                                                
      286 +| The instrument failed | The instrument's `retryable` flag | 
          +Liquid handler: no. Incubator and plate reader: yes |         
      287 +| The instrument hung | The setting `REPEAT_SAFE` | Plate read
          +er only |                                                     
      288 +| A read's data was lost | The setting `REREAD_ON_DATA_LOSS` |
          + Plate reader only |                                          
      289  
      213 -- **Limit:** at most 3 attempts in total (`MAX_ATTEMPTS`).    
      214 -- **No timers:** a hang is retried only after the instrument i
          -tself reports `hung`, so a                                    
      215 -  retry is never sent to a frozen instrument.                 
      216 -- **A step is decided by all its attempts:**                  
      217 -  - it is completed once any attempt is verified;             
      218 -  - it is failed only when every attempt has ended and no more
          - will be made.                                                
      290 +- **At most 3 attempts** per step, in total.                  
      291 +- **No timers.** A hang is retried only after the instrument i
          +tself reports `hung`, so a                                    
      292 +  retry is never sent to an instrument that is still frozen.  
      293 +- **A step is decided by all its attempts.** It is `completed`
          + once any attempt is verified,                                
      294 +  and `failed` only when every attempt has ended and no more w
          +ill be made.                                                  
      295  
      220 -**Instead of:**                                               
      221 -- *An operator decides every retry:* runs then never end on th
          -eir own. Under any fault seed                                 
      222 -  that is measured, because the fault roll is deterministic.  
      223 -- *The instrument's flag only:* a hang carries no flag, so thi
          -s can't decide the hardest                                    
      224 -  case.                                                       
      225 -- *Treating the incubator as repeat-safe:* Triple Assay would 
          -then finish under hang faults,                                
      226 -  but by incubating a plate twice for an unknown total time.  
      296 +*Example: a hang* (measured, C3). With hang faults at 30% and

      232 -### D5. Cancel stops what has not started and keeps the eviden
          -ce of what has                                                
      306 +- *An operator decides every retry:* never repeats anything by
          + itself, but under any fault

          + hardest case is left

          +ed this in review.

      234 -- **One transaction:** the cancel locks every step of the run 
          -in a fixed order.                                             
      235 -- **Sent but not claimed:** cancelled. Claim and cancel take t
          -he same lock, so exactly one                                  
      236 -  wins.                                                       
      237 -- **Claimed or running:** not stopped. Its late facts are stil
          -l recorded.                                                   
      238 -- **After the cancel:** no new attempts.                      
      239 -- **Withdrawn claim (K54):**                                  
      240 -  - The case: a claim that was granted, but the driver died be
          -fore starting.                                                
      241 -  - What happens: the restarted driver asks for the claim agai
          -n. A driver only asks before                                  
      242 -    it runs, so the second request proves the job never starte
          -d. The executor withdraws the                                 
      243 -    claim and answers `abort`.                                
      244 -  - Measured live: the ledger has nothing for that job.       
      245 -- **Run status, rule 1:**                                     
      246 -  - `completed` if every step completed;                      
      247 -  - `failed` if a step failed for its own reason;             
      248 -  - `cancelled` otherwise.                                    
      249 -- **Instead of:** "cancelled whenever a cancel was asked for".
          - That would hide a failure that                               
      250 -  happened anyway.                                            
      251 -- **Found by Codex:** the first version let a cancel relabel a
          - failure that was final anyway                                
      252 -  as "not retried: cancelled". Now the retry policy decides th
          -at, not the cancel.                                           
      313 +**Trade-offs.**                                               
      314  
      254 -### D6. GraphQL is a second view of the same rows             
      315 +- Which operations are safe to repeat on real equipment is a *
          +*guess**. That's why it is a                                  
      316 +  setting per device, not code.                               
      317 +- A retried step is two jobs in the ledger, so the acceptance 
          +script's check 5 ("no step ran                                
      318 +  more than once") fails on any run that retried. The script i
          +s meant for the happy path.                                   
      319 +- Retries go at once, with no backoff.                        
      320  
      256 -See §3.                                                       
      321 +### 2.7 Lost data stays an open question                      
      322  
      323 +**Problem.** If the driver crashes after a read but before the
          + upload succeeds, its bytes                                   
      324 +are gone. The restarted driver finds no object in storage. Sho
          +uld the read fail?                                            
      325 +                                                              
      326 +**Choice.** No. "Not in storage now" is not the same as "lost"
          +. The driver reports                                          
      327 +`data_lost` after an upload grace of 10s, and the read becomes
          + *unresolved*: the API says                                   
      328 +`running`, and `error` says the data was lost but an earlier u
          +pload may still appear. Then:                                 
      329 +                                                              
      330 +- On the plate reader, a re-read starts at once, alongside the
          + open attempt.                                                
      331 +- Every 10s the executor looks in storage for the original obj
          +ect.                                                          
      332 +- Whichever attempt is verified first completes the step.     
      333 +                                                              
      334 +*Example* (measured, C2). I stopped storage during a read, res
          +tarted the plate reader's                                     
      335 +driver once the read had completed, then started storage again
          +. The driver reported the                                     
      336 +data lost, and the run stayed `running`. I then stood in for a
          + late upload by putting the                                   
      337 +same bytes at the read's key by hand. Within one 10s check the
          + read and the run were                                        
      338 +`completed`. My first version had failed the read after a grac
          +e period. The late upload                                     
      339 +then contradicted the record, which is why this decision exist
          +s. C2 ran before re-reads                                     
      340 +existed: the re-read path is covered by unit tests, not re-run
          + live.                                                        
      341 +                                                              
      342 +**Why not make the loss final?** Storage could arbitrate with 
          +a "create only if absent"                                     
      343 +write (`If-None-Match: *`) and a tombstone. But SeaweedFS 3.62
          + ignores that header: a second                                
      344 +conditional write returned 200 and overwrote the object (measu
          +red). Nothing bounds how late                                 
      345 +a dead process's upload can land (inferred: neither S3 nor Sea
          +weedFS promises it).                                          
      346 +                                                              
      347 +**Trade-off.** If every re-read also fails and the original ne
          +ver appears, the run stays                                    
      348 +`running` for ever. It is true, but stuck (§5).               
      349 +                                                              
      350 +### 2.8 Cancel stops what hasn't started, and keeps what has  
      351 +                                                              
      352 +**Problem.** What should `CancelRun` do to each step, and what
          + is the run's status once the                                 
      353 +work still in flight ends?                                    
      354 +                                                              
      355 +**Choice.** One transaction locks every step of the run, in a 
          +fixed order so that two such                                  
      356 +transactions can't deadlock, then:                            
      357 +                                                              
      358 +| The step was… | After the cancel |                          
      359 +| --- | --- |                                                 
      360 +| completed | stays completed |                               
      361 +| claimed or running | not stopped (it may be moving liquid); 
          +its outcome is still recorded |                               
      362 +| sent, not claimed | cancelled; a later claim gets `abort` | 
      363 +| pending | cancelled; never sent |                           
      364 +| failed, and would have been retried | not retried: "not retr
          +ied: cancelled" |                                             
      365 +                                                              
      366 +*Example.* About 3s into PCR Amplification, `incubate_samples`
          + and `fill_reagent_plate` are                                 
      367 +running, and `fill_buffer_plate` is waiting for the liquid han
          +dler. After the cancel, the                                   
      368 +two running steps finish and are recorded as completed. `fill_
          +buffer_plate` and the three                                   
      369 +later steps are cancelled, and the run ends `cancelled`.      
      370 +                                                              
      371 +**The run's final status,** once nothing is in flight:        
      372 +                                                              
      373 +- `completed` if every step completed: the cancel came too lat
          +e to stop any.                                                
      374 +- `failed` if a step failed for its own reason. In the example
          +, a failure of                                                
      375 +  `fill_reagent_plate` (liquid-handler failures aren't retryab
          +le) would make it `failed`.                                   
      376 +- `cancelled` otherwise: the cancel is why the rest didn't hap
          +pen.                                                          
      377 +                                                              
      378 +**One hard case: a claim granted to a driver that then died be
          +fore starting.** The resend in                                
      379 +§2.4 would run the job once the driver is back, even after a c
          +ancel. To prevent that, a                                     
      380 +second claim for the same job, after a cancel, with no evidenc
          +e it started, is withdrawn and                                
      381 +answered `abort`. A driver asks for a claim only before runnin
          +g a job, and only for one its                                 
      382 +ledger doesn't have, so the second request itself proves the j
          +ob never started. Measured                                    
      383 +live: the step ended `cancelled`, and the ledger had nothing f
          +or that job.                                                  
      384 +                                                              
      385 +**Why not the alternatives.**                                 
      386 +                                                              
      387 +- *Call the run `cancelled` whenever a cancel was requested:* 
          +that hides a failure that                                     
      388 +  happened anyway. I rejected it in review.                   
      389 +- *Stop resending a claimed step once cancelled:* a step whose
          + driver died would then stay                                  
      390 +  `running` for ever, because the resend is the only way to le
          +arn it never started.                                         
      391 +                                                              
      392 +**Trade-off.** Work that has started is never stopped, by desi
          +gn. The withdrawn claim                                       
      393 +assumes a single executor (§5).                               
      394 +

          -y shows the attempt that

      436 +  one repeatable-read snapshot. Mutations call the same `Execu

          -ge read-back, with `matches`.                                 
      308 -    A corruption warning on a completed read then has its own 
          -field, instead of sharing

      310 -  - **`phase`:** a scientist can tell "sent, not yet started"

      311 -    "claimed" (it won't), and "storing" from "running".

      388 -### Deliberate boundaries                                     
      538 +### Not guaranteed                                            
      539  
      390 -- **Work already started is never stopped.** Not by a cancel, 
          -and not by a timer. Only its                                  
      391 -  evidence says how it ended.                                 
      392 -- **No operator tools** (K6, K39). The consequence:           
      393 -  - A read whose data was lost, and whose re-reads also failed
          - or lost their data, stays                                    
      394 -    `running` for ever.                                       
      395 -  - That is true: an earlier upload may still land, and Seawee
          -dFS can't make loss final                                     
      396 -    (D3). But a run can then never end.                       
      397 -  - The fix is designed (D4: an `abandonStep` mutation) but no
          -t built.                                                      
      398 -- **A driver that never comes back** leaves its claimed step `
          -running`. It is unknown, so                                   
      399 -  the record says so (K6).                                    
      400 -- **Times of a crashed attempt** end at the instrument's resta
          -rt, not when work actually                                    
      401 -  stopped. That is the ledger's time, the best evidence there 
          -is.                                                           
      540 +- **A run can stay `running` for ever.** Two cases: a driver t
          +hat never comes back, and a                                   
      541 +  lost read whose re-reads also fail while the original never 
          +appears. In both the record                                   
      542 +  is true (the outcome really is unknown), but nothing ends th
          +e run. I drew this boundary on                                
      543 +  purpose. The fix is an operator action ("declare lost" or "a
          +bandon this step") that marks                                 
      544 +  the step `failed` with the reason. It was designed but not b
          +uilt.                                                         
      545 +- **Work that has started is never stopped,** by a cancel or a
          + timer. Only its evidence says                                
      546 +  how it ended. This is deliberate.                           
      547 +- **Delivery is not exactly once.** Messages can arrive twice 
          +or late. Stable job ids and                                   
      548 +  idempotent apply make that harmless; no part of the design d
          +epends on exactly-once                                        
      549 +  delivery.                                                   
      550 +- **The record can lag.** By up to 30s after an executor netwo
          +rk cut, and by at least 16s                                   
      551 +  after a storage outage (measured).                          
      552  
      553  ### Assumptions the guarantees rest on
      554  
      405 -- **One driver process per instrument, and a ledger that survi
          -ves restarts (K55).**                                         
      406 -  "Resending never runs a job twice" depends on both.         
      407 -- **One executor (K56).** Withdrawing a claim after a cancel a
          -ssumes one executor answers a                                 
      408 -  driver's claims in order. With replicas, a stalled claim cou
          -ld withdraw an attempt that is                                
      409 -  running. The fix would be for drivers to number their claims
          -.                                                             
      410 -- **The instrument's end-of-job ledger write succeeds (K16).**
          - If it fails, the instrument                                  
      411 -  says `completed` now, but a restarted instrument says `hung`
          -. That can't be seen from                                     
      412 -  outside the instrument (`instrument.go:241`).               
      413 -- **A single NATS server, no retention limits on `LAB_EVENTS` 
          -(K22), and no rolling                                         
      414 -  upgrade.** A v2 driver against a v1 executor is rejected: it
          - fails safe (K14).                                            
      555 +- **One driver per instrument, and a ledger that survives rest
          +arts.** "A resend never runs a                                
      556 +  job twice" depends on both. A wiped ledger, or a second driv
          +er for the same instrument,                                   
      557 +  would break it. Not reachable in this stack (assumed).      
      558 +- **One executor.** The withdrawn claim (§2.8) assumes one exe
          +cutor answers a driver's claims                               
      559 +  in order. With two, a stalled first claim could be mistaken 
          +for a second one, and withdraw                                
      560 +  an attempt that is running. Drivers numbering their claims w
          +ould fix it.                                                  
      561 +- **The instrument's end-of-job ledger write succeeds.** If it
          + fails, the instrument reports                                
      562 +  `completed` and we record it, but after a restart its ledger
          + says `hung`                                                  
      563 +  (`instrument.go:241`, inferred). Nothing outside the instrum
          +ent can see that.                                             
      564 +- **One NATS server**, and no retention limit on the event str
          +eam. A newer driver against an                                
      565 +  older executor is rejected: safe, but no rolling upgrades.  
      566  
      567  ### Known gaps
      568  
      418 -- **`acceptance.sh` check 5** ("no step ran more than once") f
          -ails on any run that retried                                  
      419 -  (K5). Each attempt is its own ledger execution, by design. T
          -he check is meant for the                                     
      420 -  happy path, and it passes there.                            
      421 -- **REST `GET /runs/{id}` doesn't read from one snapshot (K59)
          -.** For one poll it can show a                                
      422 -  run and its steps from different moments, such as `running` 
          -beside steps that have all                                    
      423 -  ended. GraphQL reads in a snapshot.                         
      424 -- **`createRun` has no idempotency key (K58).** A tool that re
          -tries after a lost response                                   
      425 -  creates a second run.                                       
      426 -- **A partition of the executor costs up to 30s** (`ackWait`, 
          -K62). A retry after a failure                                 
      427 -  is immediate, with no backoff (K51).                        
      428 -- **Step and attempt times differ below 1µs (K61):** Postgres 
          -stores microseconds; the                                      
      429 -  lifecycle keeps the ledger's nanoseconds.                   
      569 +- **REST `GET /runs/{id}` doesn't read one snapshot.** For one
          + poll, at the moment a run                                    
      570 +  finishes, it can show the run `running` beside steps that ha
          +ve all ended. GraphQL reads one                               
      571 +  snapshot.                                                   
      572 +- **`createRun` isn't idempotent.** A client that retries afte
          +r a lost response creates a                                   
      573 +  second run.                                                 
      574 +- **Acceptance check 5 fails on any run that retried,** becaus
          +e each attempt is its own                                     
      575 +  ledger execution (§2.6).                                    
      576 +- **Retries have no backoff,** so a fault lasting more than a 
          +few seconds uses up all 3                                     
      577 +  attempts.                                                   
      578 +- **A step's times and its attempts' times differ below a micr
          +osecond:** Postgres keeps                                     
      579 +  microseconds, the ledger nanoseconds.                       
      580  
      581  ### Not proven
      582  
      433 -- **C1 can't be fully rerun from the repo.** Its probe was a l
          -ocal-only script (K8).                                        
      434 -- **C2 ran before D4.** Today a lost read is also re-read at o
          -nce. Unit tests cover that, but                               
      435 -  it hasn't been re-run live.                                 
      436 -- **Several runs competing for one device.** Chaos ran runs ba
          -ck to back, not concurrently.                                 
      437 -  There is no fairness across runs (K29).                     
      438 -- **Scale.** The dispatch loop rescans running runs every 500m
          -s, replay sends the whole                                     
      439 -  ledger on every start, and lost reads are rechecked every 10
          -s for ever. All fine at three                                 
      440 -  devices, not measured beyond (K20, K28, K35, K40, K50).     
      441 -- **One test gap (K38).** That replay uses the ledger as it wa
          -s before listening is shown by                                
      442 -  the code, not by a test.                                    
      583 +- **C1 can't be fully rerun** from the repository (its probe w
          +asn't committed).                                             
      584 +- **The re-read after lost data** is covered by unit tests, bu
          +t wasn't re-run live after C2.                                
      585 +- **Several runs competing for one device.** Chaos ran runs ba
          +ck to back, not at the same                                   
      586 +  time. There is no fairness between runs sharing a device.   
      587 +- **Scale.** The dispatch loop rescans running runs every 500m
          +s, replay resends a driver's                                  
      588 +  whole ledger on every start, and lost reads are checked ever
          +y 10s for ever. Fine with three                               
      589 +  devices; not measured beyond.

          + uses the ledger as it was                                    
      591 +  before the driver started listening, so a new read is never

⏺ I rewrote NOTES.md so it reads as an explanation rather than an investigation
  log. It's in PR #26
  (https://github.com/buildsmall-dev/automata-challenge/pull/26) for your
  review, not merged, and I changed no other files.

  Main improvements
  - It tells the story in order. It starts with what the system does, the
    problem (the record must stay true when messages and processes fail) and the
    main idea. Then it walks through the system's parts and a normal run, then
    what happens when things fail, and only then the design decisions.
  - A normal run is followed concretely. The read_plate step goes through eight
    numbered steps, with a table of what the API says at each point and what
    evidence backs it.
  - One table covers the failures. For each failure it shows what happens, what
    the record says meanwhile, and whether that was measured.
  - Each decision is written the same way: the problem, what we chose, why not
    the alternatives, and the trade-off that remains. Each has a concrete
    example:
    - claim-before-run: a cancel racing a late claim;
    - a crash after the claim: the same job is resent and runs once;
    - retries: a hang on the plate reader, which is repeated, versus the liquid
      handler, which fails with its reason;
    - lost data: the C2 experiment;
    - cancel: a PCR run cancelled about 3s in.
  - Easier to scan. There are no concern IDs in the body text, and internal
    labels like S7 and R1 are spelled out. The words measured, inferred and
    assumed are defined once at the top, then used where they matter.
  - §4 and §5 are clearer. §4 has a "Where I was wrong" section: every wrong
    prediction was about time, not truth. §5 is grouped as not guaranteed,
    assumptions, known gaps, and not proven.

  Checked: every rewritten explanation against the code and the decision, chaos
  and concern logs. All the README's required sections are still there, and the
  three placeholders for your time spent, your feedback and the AI export are
  untouched. One claim was wrong in the old draft, and I fixed it: the race
  detector caught a deliberately planted bug in a mutation test, not a real bug.

  Worth knowing before you review
  - It's longer: 632 lines, up from 476. The extra comes from the examples,
    shorter paragraphs and tables. The decisions section is where to trim if you
    want it shorter.
  - The examples come from real runs: the hang example is C3, the lost-data
    example is C2, and the crash-after-claim example is the live test in PR #18.
    Only the cancel example is illustrative, drawn from how a PCR run looks
    about 3s in, not a recorded run.
  - Merge order: the PRs are stacked #23 → #24 → #25 → #26, so merge them in
    that order.

          - such as "fill the sample

       43  ## 1. Topology
       44  
       45 -### The main idea                                             
       46 -                                                              
       47 -One rule shaped the design: **durable facts, short-lived inten
          -tions, and Postgres decides.**                                
       48 -                                                              
       49 -- **Intentions** ("run this job") travel as plain NATS request
          -s. They aren't stored anywhere                                
       50 -  on the way. If one is lost it is simply sent again. A comman
          -d must never sit in a queue                                   
       51 -  and run later, after a cancel, or after someone has moved th
          -e plate.                                                      
       52 -- **Facts** ("this job started", "it finished at 12:00:10") tr
          -avel through a JetStream                                      
       53 -  stream. They are kept until the executor has saved them, so 
          -they survive the executor                                     
       54 -  being down.                                                 
       55 -- **Postgres decides.** The executor applies every fact and ev
          -ery decision in one database                                  
       56 -  transaction. Postgres is the only place that says what shoul
          -d happen next.                                                
       57 -                                                              
       58 -### The pieces                                                
       59 -                                                              
       45  ```
       61 -     Postgres  ◄──── one transaction per fact ────  Executor  
          -── reads data back ──►  S3 storage                            
       62 -  runs, steps,                                     API, dispat
          -ch loop,                 runs/<job id>.csv                    
       63 -  every attempt                                    claims, eve
          -nts                            ▲                              
       64 -                                                    │   ▲     
          -   ▲                           │                              
       65 -                                      (1) command   │   │ (2) 
          -   │ (3) events                │                              
       66 -                                                    ▼   │ clai
          -m  │                           │                              
       67 -                                            Core NATS         
          -   JetStream LAB_EVENTS        │                              
       68 -                                       (request/reply, not    
          -  (stream, on disk)            │                              
       69 -                                             stored)          
          -           ▲                   │                              
       70 -                                                    │   ▲     
          -           │                   │ (4) upload                   
       71 -                                                    ▼   │     
          -           │                   │                              
       72 -                                     Driver (one per instrumen
          -t) ────────┴───────────────────┘                              
       73 -                                         │ in the same process
       74 -                                     Instrument ──► its ledger
          - (a file on disk; survives restarts)                          
       46 + Postgres ◄── one transaction per fact ── Executor ── reads da
          +ta back, hashes it ──► S3 storage                             
       47 + (runs, steps,                           (API, dispatch loop, 
          +                        ▲                                     
       48 +  every attempt)                          claims, events)     
          +                        │                                     
       49 +                                           │  ▲        ▲      
          +                        │                                     
       50 +                              (1) command  │  │ (2)    │ (3) e
          +vents                   │ (4) upload                          
       51 +                                           ▼  │ claim  │      
          +                        │                                     
       52 +                                     Core NATS        JetStrea
          +m LAB_EVENTS            │                                     
       53 +                                           │  ▲        ▲      
          +                        │                                     
       54 +                                           ▼  │        │      
          +                        │                                     
       55 +                                      Driver, one per instrume
          +nt ─────────────────────┘                                     
       56 +                                           │ same process     
       57 +                                      Instrument ──► ledger (f
          +ile on disk)

       97 -Every job has an id `<run>/<step>/<attempt>`, for example `run
          --3c9e/read_plate/1`. It stays                                 
       98 -the same however many times a message about it is sent, and it
          - is the unit of idempotency                                   
       99 -throughout: the driver answers `known` to a repeated command, 
          -JetStream drops a repeated                                    
      100 -event by its message id (`<job id>:<kind>`), and the storage k
          -ey comes from it.                                             
       69 +- **Duplicates.** Each event's message id is `<job id>:<kind>`
          +, so JetStream drops a repeat                                 
       70 +  within its 2-minute window. After that, applying a fact twic
          +e changes nothing.                                            
       71 +- **Events that can't be applied.** A malformed event, or one 
          +from a device that doesn't                                    
       72 +  match its subject, is rejected for good. One for a job no st
          +ep has is acked and logged. A                                 
       73 +  database error means redelivery after 1s, and an unreadable 
          +`stored` object after 2s.                                     
       74 +- **Connection health.** Both sides set NATS pings to 5s, with
          + 2 outstanding.

       90 +1. **Dispatch.** Every 500ms, or at once when a fact arrives, 
          +the executor asks Postgres                                    
       91 +   which steps are ready. It records attempt 1 (`run-3c9e/read
          +_plate/1`), then sends the

          +b id.

      101 +   `running`, with the ledger's times.*

      104 +   object back and compares its sha256 with the ledger's. *API

      137 -The executor tracks finer phases than the API shows. `dispatch
          -ed`, `claimed`, `storing`,                                    
      138 -`unresolved` and others are visible in GraphQL (§3).          
      109 +### Recovery                                                  
      110  
      140 -Every event is applied in one transaction under the step's row
          - lock, then acked. An event                                   
      141 -that can't be applied is handled by kind:                     
      111 +- **A driver restarts:** it first *replays* its ledger. It rep
          +orts how every job ended, and a                               
      112 +  job cut off by the restart is reported `hung` (the instrumen
          +t marks it so). For each                                      
      113 +  completed read, it asks storage: present → `stored`; still a
          +bsent after a 10s grace →                                     
      114 +  `data_lost`.                                                
      115 +- **A step is claimed but nothing says it started:** the execu
          +tor keeps resending the same                                  
      116 +  command every 3s (§2.3).                                    
      117 +- **The executor restarts:** its intent is in Postgres and its
          + facts wait in the stream, so                                 
      118 +  nothing in its memory needs recovering.                     
      119 +- **A read was reported lost:** every 10s the executor looks i
          +n storage for its object (§2.4).                              
      120  
      143 -- **Malformed, or from a device that doesn't match its subject
          -:** rejected for good.                                        
      144 -- **A database error:** redelivered after 1s.                 
      145 -- **A `stored` object that can't be read yet:** redelivered af
          -ter 2s.                                                       
      146 -- **For a job no step has:** acked and logged.                
      147 -                                                              
      148 -### When something goes wrong                                 
      149 -                                                              
      150 -One recovery mechanism appears in several rows. When a driver 
          -restarts, it first                                            
      151 -**replays** its ledger: it reports how every job ended, again 
          -(repeats are harmless), and                                   
      152 -checks storage for the data of each completed read. Each row i
          -s explained in §2, and most                                   
      153 -were tested in §4.                                            
      154 -                                                              
      155 -| What fails | What happens | What the record says meanwhile |
          - Evidence |                                                   
      156 -| --- | --- | --- | --- |                                     
      157 -| A command is lost, or gets no reply | Sent again with the sa
          -me job id | Unchanged | measured (C4e) |                      
      158 -| The driver dies after its claim, before starting | Resent ev
          -ery 3s. The restarted driver has no record of the job, so it a
          -ccepts it, claims it again, gets `go`, and runs it once | `run
          -ning`, no start time | measured (resend PR #18) |             
      159 -| The driver dies mid-job | On restart the instrument marks th
          -e job `hung`, ending at the restart. Replay reports it. The pl
          -ate reader repeats it; the liquid handler doesn't | `running`,
          - then the retry's outcome, or `failed` with the reason | measu
          -red (C4c, C4d) |                                              
      160 -| The instrument hangs | After 30s the ledger says `hung`. The
          -n as above | `running`, then as above | measured (C3) |       
      161 -| The driver dies before uploading a read | Replay finds no ob
          -ject, and reports `data_lost`. The read stays open, is read ag
          -ain, and storage is checked every 10s | `running`, with the re
          -ason in `error` | measured (C2), re-read by unit tests |      
      162 -| The executor is down or cut off | Events wait in JetStream. 
          -A driver asking for a claim gets no answer, so nothing starts.
          - On return it resumes from Postgres | API unavailable, then co
          -rrect but late | measured (C4a, C4b, C4f) |                   
      163 -| Storage is down | The driver retries the upload, and keeps t
          -he plate reader busy | `running` until the data is verified | 
          -measured (C4h) |
