      123  ## 2. Why this topology
      124  
      169 -### 2.1 I measured first                                      
      125 +### 2.1 Measured before designing                             
      126  
      171 -Before writing any code, I cut a bare NATS client off the netw
          -ork, with a peer that stayed                                  
      172 -connected. Every second it sent a request (2s timeout) and a J
          -etStream publish (5s timeout).                                
      173 -Runs: 6s and 20s cuts, with default pings and with 2s pings. *
          -*My prediction was wrong                                      
      174 -twice** (measured, C1):                                       
      127 +Before writing code, I cut a bare NATS client off the network 
          +for 6s and 20s, with default                                  
      128 +and with 2s pings (C1). My prediction was wrong twice (measure
          +d):                                                           
      129  
      130  - **Requests that timed out were still delivered,** up to 20s 
           late. With default pings the
      177 -  TCP connection survives the cut, so nothing was lost, but ev
          -erything was late, including                                  
      178 -  requests the sender had already given up on.                
      179 -- **7 of 8 JetStream publishes that reported a timeout had in 
          -fact been stored.**                                           
      131 +  TCP connection survived, so nothing was lost, but everything
          + was late.                                                    
      132 +- **7 of 8 JetStream publishes that reported a timeout had bee
          +n stored.**                                                   
      133  
      181 -So **a timeout means "unknown", never "it didn't happen".** An
          -ything that treats a timeout as                               
      182 -"not delivered", such as "no reply, so start a new attempt", w
          -ould sooner or later run a job                                
      183 -twice or record a false outcome. The decisions below are what 
          -follows from that.                                            
      134 +So **a timeout means "unknown", never "didn't happen"**. Any d
          +esign that turns "no reply"                                   
      135 +into "start a new attempt", or "failed", would sooner or later
          + run a job twice or record a                                  
      136 +false outcome. Every decision below follows from that.        
      137  
      185 -### 2.2 Commands as requests, facts as a stream               
      138 +### 2.2 Requests for commands, a stream for facts, Postgres de
          +cides                                                         
      139  
      187 -**Problem.** How should commands reach the drivers, and outcom
          -es come back, so that the                                     
      188 -record stays true through lost, repeated and late messages?   
      140 +A command should be fresh or not happen at all. If it is lost,
          + sending it again is harmless.                                
      141 +If it is *stored*, it can run late: after a cancel, or after s
          +omeone has moved the plate by                                 
      142 +hand. Facts are the opposite. Losing one would leave the recor
          +d wrong, so they must survive                                 
      143 +an executor that is down.                                     
      144  
      190 -**Choice.** Commands are core NATS requests. Facts go through 
          -JetStream. Postgres decides.                                  
      145 +| Alternative | Why not |                                     
      146 +| --- | --- |                                                 
      147 +| Requests only; the executor polls drivers for outcomes | Whi
          +le the executor is down, facts exist only in driver memory and
          + the ledger. It also assumes an instrument can always be asked
          +, which is true of this simulator, not of most equipment |    
      148 +| JetStream for commands too | A durable command can run late.
          + One ack timeout would also have to fit jobs of 2 seconds and 
          +of hours |                                                    
      149 +| Shared state in NATS KV | A second source of truth beside Po
          +stgres, without transactions |                                
      150  
      192 -| Option | How it works | Why not |                           
      193 -| --- | --- | --- |                                           
      194 -| **Chosen: requests for commands, a stream for facts** | as a
          -bove | |                                                      
      195 -| Requests only; the executor polls the drivers for outcomes |
          - simplest | While the executor is down, facts live only in dri
          -ver memory and the ledger. It also assumes the instrument can 
          -always be asked, which is true of this simulator but not of mo
          -st equipment |                                                
      196 -| JetStream both ways | commands in a durable queue | A stored
          - command can run late: a dispense after a cancel, or after the
          - plate was moved by hand. One ack timeout would have to fit jo
          -bs of 2 seconds and of hours |                                
      197 -| Shared state in NATS KV | drivers and executor read and writ
          -e it | A second source of truth beside Postgres, without trans
          -actions |                                                     
      151 +**Trade-offs.** Delivery is at least once, not exactly once, s
          +o every fact must apply                                       
      152 +idempotently, and it does. When the executor is cut off, event
          +s already handed to it are                                    
      153 +redelivered only after the 30s ack wait. That delays the recor
          +d, but doesn't falsify it                                     
      154 +(§4).                                                         
      155  
      199 -**Trade-offs.**                                               
      156 +### 2.3 Claim before run, and resend the same job             
      157  
      201 -- An extra round trip per job, for the claim (§2.3).          
      202 -- JetStream delivers **at least once, not exactly once.** A re
          -peat within 2 minutes is                                      
      203 -  dropped by its message id. After that, applying the same fac
          -t twice changes nothing,                                      
      204 -  because each fact is applied idempotently.                  
      205 -- When the executor is cut off, events in flight are redeliver
          -ed only after the 30s ack                                     
      206 -  timeout. An 8s cut delayed a run by 30s (measured, C4f). The
          - record was behind, never                                     
      207 -  false.                                                      
      158 +**Claim.** A late command, or a cancel arriving at any moment,
          + must never lead to a job                                     
      159 +starting after its cancel. The driver's claim and the cancel t
          +ake the same row lock, so                                     
      160 +exactly one of them is first:                                 
      161  
      209 -### 2.3 Claim before run                                      
      162 +- if the cancel is first, the claim gets `abort`;             
      163 +- if the claim is first, the job may run, and its outcome is r
          +ecorded when it arrives.                                      
      164  
      211 -**Problem.** A command can arrive late, and a cancel can arriv
          -e at any moment. How do we                                    
      212 -make sure a job never starts after it was cancelled?          
      165 +The rejected alternatives, commands that expire or a broadcast
          + cancel, only narrow the                                      
      166 +window. They still race with the cancel, and expiry depends on
          + synchronised clocks.                                         
      167  
      214 -**Choice.** Before calling the instrument, the driver asks the
          - executor for permission (a                                   
      215 -*claim*). The executor records it under the step's row lock, t
          -he same lock a cancel takes.                                  
      216 -Exactly one of them gets the lock first:                      
      168 +**Resend.** A step can be claimed, then go silent: the driver 
          +died before starting, or the                                  
      169 +instrument refused the job. I first had the executor stop send
          +ing once a step was claimed.                                  
      170 +To reproduce the problem (PR #18, measured), I held the step's
          + row lock in `psql` so that                                   
      171 +`liquid-handler-1`'s claim for `fill_sample_plate` was stuck i
          +n the executor. Then I killed                                 
      172 +the driver and released the lock. The claim committed, and its
          + `go` went to a dead inbox.                                   
      173 +With nothing in the ledger, no event would ever come: the run 
          +was still `running` 20s                                       
      174 +after the driver restarted.                                   
      175  
      218 -- **The cancel first.** The claim is answered `abort`, and the
          - job never runs.                                              
      219 -- **The claim first.** The job may run. The cancel no longer s
          -tops it, because the driver                                   
      220 -  may already be starting it. Its outcome is recorded when the
          - events arrive.                                               
      176 +The fix is to keep resending the same command while a step is 
          +claimed with no evidence, and                                 
      177 +to read the driver's answer as evidence:                      
      178  
      222 -If the executor doesn't answer (it is down, or the database fa
          -iled), the driver doesn't                                     
      223 -start. It asks again.                                         
      179 +- **`known`:** the driver has the job, in flight or in its led
          +ger.                                                          
      180 +- **`accepted`:** the driver has no record of it, so it never 
          +started (the ledger is written                                
      181 +  before any physical work). It claims again, gets `go`, and r
          +uns it once.                                                  
      182 +- **No reply:** still unknown, so the step stays `running`.   
      183  
      225 -*Example.* A run is cancelled while `fill_buffer_plate` waits 
          -for the busy liquid handler.                                  
      226 -The liquid handler frees up a moment before the cancel, and th
          -e driver accepts the command.                                 
      227 -Its claim arrives just after the cancel commits, so it is answ
          -ered `abort`. The ledger never                                
      228 -shows the job.                                                
      184 +After the fix, the reproduced run completed. The job started 1
          +.4s after the driver                                          
      185 +restarted, and its ledger has exactly one execution. A separat
          +e "what do you know about this                                
      186 +job?" query was the alternative, but the command already asks 
          +exactly that.                                                 
      187  
      230 -**Why not the alternatives.** Commands that expire, or a broad
          -cast "cancel" message, only                                   
      231 -make the window for a late command smaller. Both still race wi
          -th the cancel, and expiry also                                
      232 -depends on synchronised clocks. The lock closes the window.   
      188 +**Trade-offs.** The claim costs one extra round trip per job. 
          +A driver that never comes back                                
      189 +leaves its step `running` for ever (§5). The guarantee rests o
          +n one driver per instrument,                                  
      190 +with a ledger that survives restarts, and on one executor answ
          +ering claims (assumed).                                       
      191  
      234 -**Trade-off.** It rests on one executor answering claims (assu
          -med; see §5).                                                 
      192 +### 2.4 Data counts only once verified; lost data stays open  
      193  
      236 -### 2.4 A repeated message never becomes a new attempt        
      194 +A read's bytes reach the driver only in memory, so the ledger 
          +saying `completed` is not                                     
      195 +enough. The executor completes a read only after reading the o
          +bject back and matching the                                   
      196 +hash (§1). Once verified, a read stays `completed`. A later mi
          +smatch is reported in `error`,                                
      197 +because moving the status backwards would also undermine the s
          +teps that already used the                                    
      198 +data.                                                         
      199  
      238 -**Problem.** A step is claimed, but no `started` arrives. Did 
          -the driver die before starting,                               
      239 -did the instrument refuse the job, or is the event just late? 
          -Guessing wrong either loses a                                 
      240 -job or runs it twice.                                         
      200 +The harder case is a driver that crashes before its upload suc
          +ceeds. The restarted driver                                   
      201 +finds no object, and reports `data_lost`. My first version fai
          +led the read after a grace                                    
      202 +period. **C2 showed why that was wrong** (measured). I stopped
          + storage mid-read, restarted                                  
      203 +the driver, started storage again, then put the read's bytes a
          +t its key by hand, standing in                                
      204 +for a late upload. The run had already been failed, so the rec
          +ord now contradicted storage.                                 
      205  
      242 -**Choice.** Keep sending the **same command, with the same job
          - id**, every 3s. The driver's                                 
      243 -answer is the evidence:                                       
      206 +So a lost read is now *unresolved*. The API says `running`, an
          +d `error` says an earlier upload                              
      207 +may still appear. The plate reader reads the plate again at on
          +ce, the executor checks storage                               
      208 +for the original every 10s, and whichever attempt is verified 
          +first completes the step.                                     
      209 +After the change, C2's late upload completed the run within on
          +e check.                                                      
      210  
      245 -- **`known`:** the driver has the job, in flight or in its led
          -ger. It ran, or is running.                                   
      246 -  Wait for its events.                                        
      247 -- **`accepted`:** the driver has no record of it. The instrume
          -nt writes its ledger before                                   
      248 -  doing anything physical (inferred, `instrument.go`), so the 
          -job never started. The driver                                 
      249 -  claims it again, the executor answers `go`, and it runs once
          -.                                                             
      250 -- **No answer:** still unknown. The step stays `running` and t
          -he command is sent again.                                     
      211 +Making loss final would need storage to refuse a late write. S
          +eaweedFS 3.62 ignores                                         
      212 +`If-None-Match: *`: a second conditional PUT returned 200 and 
          +overwrote the object                                          
      213 +(measured). **Trade-off:** if every re-read also fails and the
          + original never appears, the                                  
      214 +run stays `running` (§5). C2 ran before re-reads existed, so t
          +hat path is covered by unit                                   
      215 +tests, not a live run.                                        
      216  
      252 -*Example* (measured, PR #18): the plate-reader driver was kill
          -ed after its claim was                                        
      253 -granted, before it started the job. The step said `running` wi
          -th no start time. After the                                   
      254 -restart the resend was accepted, and the ledger shows the job 
          -ran exactly once.                                             
      217 +### 2.5 Retry only with a positive reason                     
      218  
      256 -A new attempt, with a new job id, is only ever started by the 
          -retry policy (§2.6), never by                                 
      257 -a timeout. I first designed a separate "what do you know about
          - this job?" query. The command                                
      258 -already asks exactly that, so the query would have added a sub
          -ject and driver code to learn                                 
      259 -nothing more.                                                 
      219 +Without retries, every fault fails the run. A careless retry, 
          +though, could dispense liquid                                 
      220 +twice into the same plate. So a new attempt needs a statement 
          +that repeating is safe, from                                  
      221 +the right source for its cause:                               
      222  
      261 -**Trade-off.** If a driver never comes back, its step stays `r
          -unning` for ever: we can't                                    
      262 -know whether it ran. An operator action to declare it lost was
          - not built (§5).                                              
      263 -                                                              
      264 -### 2.5 A read is complete only when its data is verified     
      265 -                                                              
      266 -**Problem.** The instrument hands a read's bytes to the driver
          - only in memory. The ledger                                   
      267 -saying `completed` doesn't mean the data is safe.             
      268 -                                                              
      269 -**Choice.** A read is `completed` only when the executor has r
          -ead the object back from the                                  
      270 -key it computes itself, and its sha256 equals the ledger's. Un
          -til then the step is `running`.                               
      271 -The driver keeps the plate reader busy until the upload succee
          -ds, so its bytes aren't                                       
      272 -dropped.                                                      
      273 -                                                              
      274 -**Once verified, it stays verified.** If a later read-back doe
          -sn't match (say the object was                                
      275 -corrupted), the step stays `completed` and the mismatch is rep
          -orted in `error`. Moving the                                  
      276 -status backwards would be true, but steps after it may already
          - have used that data.                                         
      277 -                                                              
      278 -### 2.6 Retry only when repeating is known to be safe         
      279 -                                                              
      280 -**Problem.** Some steps can't succeed as they stand: the instr
          -ument failed, hung, or a read's                               
      281 -data was lost. Without retries, every fault fails the run. A c
          -areless retry, though, could                                  
      282 -dispense liquid twice into the same plate.                    
      283 -                                                              
      284 -**Choice.** A new attempt needs a positive reason to think rep
          -eating is safe, from the right                                
      285 -source for its cause:                                         
      286 -                                                              
      287 -| Cause | Who says repeating is safe | Default |              
      223 +| Cause | Safe to repeat if | Default |                       
      224  | --- | --- | --- |
      289 -| The instrument failed | The instrument's `retryable` flag | 
          -Liquid handler: no. Incubator and plate reader: yes |         
      290 -| The instrument hung | The setting `REPEAT_SAFE` | Plate read
          -er only |                                                     
      291 -| A read's data was lost | The setting `REREAD_ON_DATA_LOSS` |
          - Plate reader only |                                          
      225 +| The instrument failed | the instrument marks the failure `re
          +tryable` | liquid handler no; incubator and plate reader yes |
      226 +| The instrument hung | the device is in `REPEAT_SAFE` | plate
          + reader only |                                                
      227 +| A read's data was lost | the device is in `REREAD_ON_DATA_LO
          +SS` | plate reader only |                                     
      228  
      293 -- **At most 3 attempts** per step, in total.                  
      294 -- **No timers.** A hang is retried only after the instrument i
          -tself reports `hung`, so a                                    
      295 -  retry is never sent to an instrument that is still frozen.  
      296 -- **A step is decided by all its attempts.** It is `completed`
          - once any attempt is verified,                                
      297 -  and `failed` only when every attempt has ended and no more w
          -ill be made.                                                  
      229 +- At most 3 attempts per step.                                
      230 +- **No timers:** a hang is retried only after the instrument i
          +tself reports `hung`, so a retry                              
      231 +  never goes to an instrument that is still frozen.           
      232 +- **A step is decided by all its attempts:** `completed` once 
          +any attempt is verified;                                      
      233 +  `failed` only when every attempt has ended and none will fol
          +low.                                                          
      234  
      299 -*Example: a hang* (measured, C3). With hang faults at 30% and 
          -seed 17, `plate_read` hung on                                 
      300 -its first attempt. Thirty seconds later the ledger said `hung`
          -. Attempt 2 was sent 31ms after                               
      301 -that, completed, and decided the step. The run took 36s instea
          -d of about 6s. With seed 0,                                   
      302 -`fill_sample_plate` hung on the liquid handler instead. It was
          -n't repeated, and the step                                    
      303 -failed with "instrument hung; outcome unknown; not repeated: l
          -iquid-handler-1 is not safe to                                
      304 -repeat". The other six steps were never sent. That is the hone
          -st answer: a hung dispense                                    
      305 -may have moved some, all or none of the liquid.               
      235 +In C3 (measured) a hung plate read was repeated 31ms after the
          + ledger reported the hang, and                                
      236 +completed. A hung `fill_sample_plate` on the liquid handler fa
          +iled instead: "instrument hung;                               
      237 +outcome unknown; not repeated: liquid-handler-1 is not safe to
          + repeat". A hung dispense may                                 
      238 +have moved some, all or none of the liquid.                   
      239  
      307 -**Why not the alternatives.**                                 
      240 +**Alternatives.** An operator could decide every retry, but th
          +en runs never end by                                          
      241 +themselves under faults. The instrument's flag alone can't dec
          +ide a hang, because a hang has                                
      242 +no flag. Treating the incubator as safe to repeat would let ru
          +ns finish, but by incubating a                                
      243 +plate twice for an unknown total time.                        
      244  
      309 -- *An operator decides every retry:* never repeats anything by
          - itself, but under any fault                                  
      310 -  setting, runs never end on their own.                       
      311 -- *Use only the instrument's flag:* a hang has no flag, so the
          - hardest case is left                                         
      312 -  undecided.                                                  
      313 -- *Treat the incubator as safe to repeat:* runs would finish u
          -nder hang faults, but by                                      
      314 -  incubating a plate twice for an unknown total time. I reject
          -ed this in review.                                            
      245 +**Trade-offs.** Which operations are safe to repeat on real eq
          +uipment is a guess, so it is a                                
      246 +setting per device, not code. Retries have no backoff. A retri
          +ed step is two jobs in its                                    
      247 +ledger, so the acceptance script's check 5 ("no step ran more 
          +than once") fails on such runs,                               
      248 +by design.                                                    
      249  
      316 -**Trade-offs.**                                               
      250 +### 2.6 Cancel stops what hasn't started, and keeps what has  
      251  
      318 -- Which operations are safe to repeat on real equipment is a *
          -*guess**. That's why it is a                                  
      319 -  setting per device, not code.                               
      320 -- A retried step is two jobs in the ledger, so the acceptance 
          -script's check 5 ("no step ran                                
      321 -  more than once") fails on any run that retried. The script i
          -s meant for the happy path.                                   
      322 -- Retries go at once, with no backoff.                        
      323 -                                                              
      324 -### 2.7 Lost data stays an open question                      
      325 -                                                              
      326 -**Problem.** If the driver crashes after a read but before the
          - upload succeeds, its bytes                                   
      327 -are gone. The restarted driver finds no object in storage. Sho
          -uld the read fail?                                            
      328 -                                                              
      329 -**Choice.** No. "Not in storage now" is not the same as "lost"
          -. The driver reports                                          
      330 -`data_lost` after an upload grace of 10s, and the read becomes
          - *unresolved*: the API says                                   
      331 -`running`, and `error` says the data was lost but an earlier u
          -pload may still appear. Then:                                 
      332 -                                                              
      333 -- On the plate reader, a re-read starts at once, alongside the
          - open attempt.                                                
      334 -- Every 10s the executor looks in storage for the original obj
          -ect.                                                          
      335 -- Whichever attempt is verified first completes the step.     
      336 -                                                              
      337 -*Example* (measured, C2). I stopped storage during a read, res
          -tarted the plate reader's                                     
      338 -driver once the read had completed, then started storage again
          -. The driver reported the                                     
      339 -data lost, and the run stayed `running`. I then stood in for a
          - late upload by putting the                                   
      340 -same bytes at the read's key by hand. Within one 10s check the
          - read and the run were                                        
      341 -`completed`. My first version had failed the read after a grac
          -e period. The late upload                                     
      342 -then contradicted the record, which is why this decision exist
          -s. C2 ran before re-reads                                     
      343 -existed: the re-read path is covered by unit tests, not re-run
          - live.                                                        
      344 -                                                              
      345 -**Why not make the loss final?** Storage could arbitrate with 
          -a "create only if absent"                                     
      346 -write (`If-None-Match: *`) and a tombstone. But SeaweedFS 3.62
          - ignores that header: a second                                
      347 -conditional write returned 200 and overwrote the object (measu
          -red). Nothing bounds how late                                 
      348 -a dead process's upload can land (inferred: neither S3 nor Sea
          -weedFS promises it).                                          
      349 -                                                              
      350 -**Trade-off.** If every re-read also fails and the original ne
          -ver appears, the run stays                                    
      351 -`running` for ever. It is true, but stuck (§5).               
      352 -                                                              
      353 -### 2.8 Cancel stops what hasn't started, and keeps what has  
      354 -                                                              
      355 -**Problem.** What should `CancelRun` do to each step, and what
          - is the run's status once the                                 
      356 -work still in flight ends?                                    
      357 -                                                              
      358 -**Choice.** One transaction locks every step of the run, in a 
          -fixed order so that two such                                  
      252 +`CancelRun` is one transaction. It locks every step of the run
          + in a fixed order, so two such                                
      253  transactions can't deadlock, then:
      254  
      361 -| The step was… | After the cancel |                          
      362 -| --- | --- |                                                 
      363 -| completed | stays completed |                               
      364 -| claimed or running | not stopped (it may be moving liquid); 
          -its outcome is still recorded |                               
      365 -| sent, not claimed | cancelled; a later claim gets `abort` | 
      366 -| pending | cancelled; never sent |                           
      367 -| failed, and would have been retried | not retried: "not retr
          -ied: cancelled" |                                             
      255 +- a step sent but not claimed is cancelled, and a later claim 
          +for it gets `abort`;                                          
      256 +- a step not yet sent is cancelled, and is never sent;        
      257 +- a claimed or running step is **not** stopped (it may be movi
          +ng liquid), and its outcome is                                
      258 +  still recorded;                                             
      259 +- nothing is retried after the cancel.                        
      260  
      369 -*Example.* About 3s into PCR Amplification, `incubate_samples`
          - and `fill_reagent_plate` are                                 
      370 -running, and `fill_buffer_plate` is waiting for the liquid han
          -dler. After the cancel, the                                   
      371 -two running steps finish and are recorded as completed. `fill_
          -buffer_plate` and the three                                   
      372 -later steps are cancelled, and the run ends `cancelled`.      
      261 +Once nothing is in flight, the run is `completed` if every ste
          +p completed, `failed` if a step                               
      262 +failed for its own reason, and `cancelled` otherwise. I reject
          +ed "cancelled whenever a cancel                               
      263 +was requested", because it would hide a failure that happened 
          +anyway.                                                       
      264  
      374 -**The run's final status,** once nothing is in flight:        
      265 +**One hard case:** a claim granted to a driver that then died 
          +before starting. The resend                                   
      266 +(§2.3) would run that job once the driver is back, even after 
          +a cancel. So a second claim for                               
      267 +the same job, after a cancel, with no evidence it started, is 
          +withdrawn and answered `abort`.                               
      268 +A driver asks for a claim only before running a job its ledger
          + doesn't have, so the second                                  
      269 +request itself proves the job never started. Measured live: th
          +e step ended `cancelled`, with                                
      270 +nothing in the ledger. This relies on one executor answering c
          +laims in order (assumed, §5).                                 
      271  
      376 -- `completed` if every step completed: the cancel came too lat
          -e to stop any.                                                
      377 -- `failed` if a step failed for its own reason. In the example
          -, a failure of                                                
      378 -  `fill_reagent_plate` (liquid-handler failures aren't retryab
          -le) would make it `failed`.                                   
      379 -- `cancelled` otherwise: the cancel is why the rest didn't hap
          -pen.                                                          
      380 -                                                              
      381 -**One hard case: a claim granted to a driver that then died be
          -fore starting.** The resend in                                
      382 -§2.4 would run the job once the driver is back, even after a c
          -ancel. To prevent that, a                                     
      383 -second claim for the same job, after a cancel, with no evidenc
          -e it started, is withdrawn and                                
      384 -answered `abort`. A driver asks for a claim only before runnin
          -g a job, and only for one its                                 
      385 -ledger doesn't have, so the second request itself proves the j
          -ob never started. Measured                                    
      386 -live: the step ended `cancelled`, and the ledger had nothing f
          -or that job.                                                  
      387 -                                                              
      388 -**Why not the alternatives.**                                 
      389 -                                                              
      390 -- *Call the run `cancelled` whenever a cancel was requested:* 
          -that hides a failure that                                     
      391 -  happened anyway. I rejected it in review.                   
      392 -- *Stop resending a claimed step once cancelled:* a step whose
          - driver died would then stay                                  
      393 -  `running` for ever, because the resend is the only way to le
          -arn it never started.                                         
      394 -                                                              
      395 -**Trade-off.** Work that has started is never stopped, by desi
          -gn. The withdrawn claim                                       
      396 -assumes a single executor (§5).

      274  ## 3. GraphQL schema
      275  
      402 -`POST /graphql` on port 5002. The REST API is unchanged. The f
          -ull schema, with field                                        
      403 -descriptions, is at the top of `services/executor/graphql.go`.
          - In short:                                                    
      276 +`POST /graphql` on port 5002, alongside the unchanged REST API
          +. The full schema, with field                                 
      277 +descriptions, is in `services/executor/graphql.go`:           
      278  
      279  ```graphql
      406 -type Query {                                                  
      407 -  run(id: ID!): Run                       # null if unknown   
      408 -  runs(status: RunStatus): [Run!]!        # newest first      
      409 -  workflows: [Workflow!]!                                     
      410 -}                                                             
      411 -type Mutation {                                               
      412 -  createRun(workflowName: String): Run!                       
      413 -  startRun(id: ID!): Run!                                     
      414 -  cancelRun(id: ID!): Run!                                    
      415 -}                                                             
      280 +type Query    { run(id: ID!): Run   runs(status: RunStatus): [
          +Run!]!   workflows: [Workflow!]! }                            
      281 +type Mutation { createRun(workflowName: String): Run!   startR
          +un(id: ID!): Run!   cancelRun(id: ID!): Run! }                
      282 +                                                              
      283  type Run  { id workflowName status createdAt startedAt finishe
           dAt
      417 -            cancelRequested: Boolean!     # may still be runni
          -ng: claimed work isn't stopped                                
      418 -            steps: [Step!]! }                                 
      419 -type Step { name device dependsOn status   # status and times 
          -exactly as REST                                               
      420 -            phase: StepPhase!             # DISPATCHED CLAIMED
          - RUNNING STORING UNRESOLVED ENDED …                           
      421 -            startedAt finishedAt error dataRef                
      422 -            attempts: [Attempt!]! }       # every attempt, fir
          -st first                                                      
      284 +            cancelRequested: Boolean!   steps: [Step!]! }     
      285 +type Step { name device dependsOn status startedAt finishedAt 
          +error dataRef   # exactly as REST                             
      286 +            phase: StepPhase!           # DISPATCHED CLAIMED R
          +UNNING STORING UNRESOLVED ENDED …                             
      287 +            attempts: [Attempt!]! }                           
      288  type Attempt { number jobId phase decided claimed
      424 -               outcome: Outcome           # COMPLETED FAILED H
          -UNG; null until the ledger says                               
      425 -               startedAt finishedAt       # the ledger's times
          - for this attempt                                             
      289 +               outcome startedAt finishedAt          # the led
          +ger's, for this attempt                                       
      290                 instrumentError retryable unresolved
      427 -               data: AttemptData }        # reads only        
      291 +               data: AttemptData }                   # reads o
          +nly                                                           
      292  type AttemptData  { ledgerSha256 verified dataLost checks: [St
           orageCheck!]! }
      429 -type StorageCheck { ref sha256 matches at }  # `at` is the exe
          -cutor's clock, not the ledger's                               
      293 +type StorageCheck { ref sha256 matches at }                   
      294  ```
      295  
      296  Errors carry `extensions.code`: `NOT_FOUND`, `BAD_USER_INPUT`,
            `WORKFLOWS_INVALID` or
      433 -`INTERNAL`. As in REST, internal detail goes only to the log. 
      297 +`INTERNAL`.                                                   
      298  
      435 -**Why this shape.**                                           
      299 +**Why this shape:**                                           
      300  
      437 -- **It can't contradict REST.** It keeps no state of its own. 
          -A step's fields are the same                                  
      438 -  columns REST reads, and `attempts` comes from the same row. 
          -A run and its steps are read in                               
      439 -  one repeatable-read snapshot. Mutations call the same `Execu
          -tor` methods.                                                 
      440 -- **It shows what REST's fixed shape can't:**                 
      441 -  - **Every attempt, with its own ledger times.** After a retr
          -y, REST shows only the attempt                                
      442 -    that decided the step. In C3 a hung first read held the pl
          -ate reader for 30s, and REST                                  
      443 -    didn't show it. Measured: three hung attempts in GraphQL m
          -atched three ledger                                           
      444 -    executions, while REST's `/timeline` showed only the last.
      445 -  - **The data evidence:** the ledger's hash and every storage
          - read-back, with `matches`.                                   
      446 -    A warning on a completed read gets its own field, instead 
          -of sharing REST's `error`.                                    
      447 -  - **`phase`:** tells "sent, not yet claimed" (a cancel still
          - stops it) from "claimed" (it                                 
      448 -    won't), and "storing" from "running".                     
      449 -- **Kept small.** Three queries and three mutations: enough to
          - start runs and follow them.                                  
      450 -  No subscriptions, so clients poll `run(id)`. A subscription 
          -would need a websocket                                        
      451 -  transport and a change feed the executor doesn't have, and p
          -olling is enough for runs                                     
      452 -  that take seconds to minutes. No pagination yet.            
      453 -- **Library: `graph-gophers/graphql-go`.** It is schema-first,
          - so the schema is the design,                                 
      454 -  and the resolvers are plain struct fields. gqlgen would have
          - added a code generator and                                   
      455 -  thousands of generated lines.                               
      301 +- **It can't contradict REST.** It has no state of its own. St
          +eps are the same rows REST                                    
      302 +  reads, and a run and its steps are read in one repeatable-re
          +ad snapshot. Mutations call the                               
      303 +  same `Executor` methods.                                    
      304 +- **It adds what REST's fixed shape can't show:**             
      305 +  - Every attempt, with its own ledger times. After a retry, R
          +EST shows only the attempt that                               
      306 +    decided the step, so in C3 a 30s hung read on the plate re
          +ader was invisible. Measured:                                 
      307 +    three hung attempts in GraphQL matched three ledger execut
          +ions.                                                         
      308 +  - For a read, the ledger's hash and every storage read-back,
          + with `matches`.                                              
      309 +  - The internal `phase`, which tells "sent" (a cancel still s
          +tops it) from "claimed" (it                                   
      310 +    won't).                                                   
      311 +- **Small, and polling:** three queries and three mutations, e
          +nough to start runs and follow                                
      312 +  them. Subscriptions would need a websocket transport and a c
          +hange feed the executor doesn't                               
      313 +  have, and runs take seconds to minutes.                     
      314 +- **`graph-gophers/graphql-go`,** schema-first, so the schema 
          +is the design and resolvers are                               
      315 +  plain struct fields. gqlgen would add a code generator and t
          +housands of generated lines.

      319  ## 4. What I did to break it
      320  
      461 -### Method                                                    
      321 +Each experiment: write the prediction down first, inject the f
          +ault, compare the record with                                 
      322 +every ledger and with storage, then log where the prediction w
          +as wrong. From C4 on,                                         
      323 +`scripts/check-run.py` does the comparison. It checks outcomes
          + and times against the ledgers,                               
      324 +that every ledger execution is an attempt in the record, that 
          +no job ran twice, and that every                              
      325 +completed read's object has the ledger's hash. I tested the ch
          +ecker against six kinds of                                    
      326 +doctored ledger; it caught all six.                           
      327  
      463 -Every experiment followed the same four steps:                
      328 +The instrument picks faults from `sha256(seed:device:step:atte
          +mpt)`, with no run id. So                                     
      329 +under one seed every run meets the same faults, and only a ret
          +ry rolls again (measured). I                                  
      330 +used this to choose seeds that exercise a given path.         
      331  
      465 -1. **Predict**, in writing, before running.                   
      466 -2. **Inject the fault** at a chosen moment.                   
      467 -3. **Compare** the record with every ledger and with storage. 
      468 -4. **Log it**, including where the prediction was wrong.      
      469 -                                                              
      470 -From C4 on, `scripts/check-run.py` does step 3. For each run i
          -t checks that every outcome                                   
      471 -and time matches a ledger entry, every ledger execution is an 
          -attempt in the record, no job                                 
      472 -ran twice, and every completed read's object has the ledger's 
          -hash. I tested the checker                                    
      473 -against six kinds of doctored ledger, and it caught all six.  
      474 -                                                              
      475 -A tip that made predictions exact: the instrument picks faults
          - from                                                         
      476 -`sha256(seed:device:step:attempt)`, with no run id in it. So u
          -nder one seed every run meets                                 
      477 -the same faults, and only a retry rolls again (measured). Comp
          -uting the roll before running                                 
      478 -tells you which steps will fail or hang.                      
      479 -

      542 -### Not guaranteed                                            
      368 +**Boundaries I drew on purpose:**                             
      369  
      544 -- **A run can stay `running` for ever.** Two cases: a driver t
          -hat never comes back, and a                                   
      545 -  lost read whose re-reads also fail while the original never 
          -appears. In both the record                                   
      546 -  is true (the outcome really is unknown), but nothing ends th
          -e run. I drew this boundary on                                
      547 -  purpose. The fix is an operator action ("declare lost" or "a
          -bandon this step") that marks                                 
      548 -  the step `failed` with the reason. It was designed but not b
          -uilt.                                                         
      549 -- **Work that has started is never stopped,** by a cancel or a
          - timer. Only its evidence says                                
      550 -  how it ended. This is deliberate.                           
      551 -- **Delivery is not exactly once.** Messages can arrive twice 
          -or late. Stable job ids and                                   
      552 -  idempotent apply make that harmless; no part of the design d
          -epends on exactly-once                                        
      553 -  delivery.                                                   
      554 -- **The record can lag.** By up to 30s after an executor netwo
          -rk cut, and by at least 16s                                   
      555 -  after a storage outage (measured).                          
      370 +- **A run can stay `running` for ever** when the outcome is tr
          +uly unknown: a driver that

      376 +**Assumptions:**

      559 -- **One driver per instrument, and a ledger that survives rest
          -arts.** "A resend never runs a                                
      560 -  job twice" depends on both. A wiped ledger, or a second driv
          -er for the same instrument,                                   
      561 -  would break it. Not reachable in this stack (assumed).      
      562 -- **One executor.** The withdrawn claim (§2.8) assumes one exe
          -cutor answers a driver's claims                               
      563 -  in order. With two, a stalled first claim could be mistaken 
          -for a second one, and withdraw                                
      564 -  an attempt that is running. Drivers numbering their claims w
          -ould fix it.

          - fails, the instrument reports

          -ent can see that.

          -eam. A newer driver against an                                
      569 -  older executor is rejected: safe, but no rolling upgrades.  
      378 +- **One driver per instrument, with a ledger that survives res

      574 -  finishes, it can show the run `running` beside steps that ha
          -ve all ended. GraphQL reads one                               
      575 -  snapshot.                                                   
      576 -- **`createRun` isn't idempotent.** A client that retries afte
          -r a lost response creates a                                   
      577 -  second run.

          -e each attempt is its own

          -few seconds uses up all 3

      583 -  microseconds, the ledger nanoseconds.

    To get closer to half I'd shorten §2.5 (retries) and §5 (limitations), but
    both carry reasoning I'd rather keep. Say if you want that cut anyway.

    acks; why this design; GraphQL; breaking it; limitations and assumptions;
    time spent; feedback). Your three placeholders are untouched.

  Facts corrected against the code and logs
  - The S7 crash example was wrong. The previous drafts said the plate reader.
    The PR #18 reproduction actually used liquid-handler-1 on fill_sample_plate.
    I held the step's lock in psql, killed the driver, then released the lock,
    so the claim's go reply went to a process that was already dead. Before the
    fix, the run was still running 20s after the driver restarted. After it, the
    job started 1.4s after the restart and ran exactly once.
  - Storage recovery timing was overstated. It was measured once (C4h): a 15s
    outage, after which the read completed 17s after storage came back, because
    SeaweedFS took about 16s to serve S3 again. That shorter outages pay the
    same cost is now marked as inferred; it was never measured.
  - The C2 story was blurred. C2 tested the new lost-data rule, and the read
    stayed open until the late upload appeared. The record contradicting storage
    happened earlier, when the same sequence ran on the first version.

    explainers.
  - It says Codex reviewed each slice PR. The /break PR (#21) and the decision
    PRs had no Codex review, so "each code PR" was too strong.

  /decide, /slice, and /break skills to establish how we wanted to work on this

  Explain briefly why we created them and how they shaped the implementation:   
                                                                                
  /decide helped us examine alternatives, question assumptions, and use         
  evidence before choosing a design.                                            
                                                                                
  /slice encouraged small, test-first changes with focused PRs and independent  
  code review.

  run record with the actual instrument ledgers and storage.

  They helped keep the work disciplined and aligned with our main principle:    
  the run record must be true.

  have the same types, fields, arguments and enum values.

  Project skills: the AI use section now opens with a short part called "How we
  set up the work":
  - Why: CLAUDE.md and the skills came before any code because the risk with
    AI-written code is plausible claims nobody has checked, the same failure the
    run record must avoid. CLAUDE.md states the main principle and a review
    checklist: what if a message arrives twice, late or never, or the process
    restarts halfway?
  - /decide: real alternatives and their assumptions, with facts settled by
    experiment before choosing. The examples are C1 before the messaging design,
    and measuring that faults don't depend on the run before the retry design.
  - /slice: one behaviour per PR, starting from a failing check, then the race
    detector, the acceptance script, one Codex review, and an explanation you
    checked before merging.
  - /break: a written prediction first, then the record compared with the
    ledgers and storage. That comparison became check-run.py. Wrong predictions
    were logged as findings, such as the 30s ack wait.
  - It closes on your point: none of this was about generating code faster; it
    kept each claim tied to evidence.

  "Who did what".
